<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the annular <i>r</i>-variation estimate for the triangular Hilbert transform from complex $`L^3\times L^3`$ to <i>L</i><sup>3/2</sup> for every <i>r</i> &gt; 2. The partitions may depend on the output point and range over all positive scales. The estimate yields the two-endpoint maximal bound and joint almost-everywhere and norm principal values, and resolves the symmetric scalar triangular Hilbert transform problem.

- **claim-full-annular-variation** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-variation)): For every real $r>2$ there is a finite constant $C_r$ such that $$\\|V_r(F,G)\\|_{L^{3/2}(\mathbb R^2)} \le C_r\\|F\\|_{L^3(\mathbb R^2)}\\|G\\|_{L^3(\mathbb R^2)}$$ for all complex $F,G\in L^3(\mathbb R^2)$.
  - Explanation: Controls annular variation with output-dependent partitions over all positive scales at the symmetric Lebesgue exponents.
- **claim-disjoint-annulus-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-count)): There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, for every integer $n\ge1$ and every complex $F,G\in L^3(\mathbb R^2)$, $$\label{eq:count-bound} \\|\mathcal S_n(F,G)\\|_{3/2} \le C\sqrt n\log(2+n)\\|F\\|_3\\|G\\|_3.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the pointwise supremum of sums of disjoint annular increments with a square-root count loss and a logarithmic factor.
- **claim-finite-truncation-representatives** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-truncations)): For $F,G\in L^3(\mathbb R^2)$, all finite annular integrals defining $B_{\varepsilon,R}(F,G)$ are absolutely convergent outside one common null set. On its complement they depend continuously on $(\varepsilon,R)$ in $0<\varepsilon<R<\infty$. Changing representatives changes this family only on a common null set. Moreover, $$\label{eq:finite-annulus-bound} \left\\|\int_{\varepsilon<\|t\|<R} \|F(x+t,y)G(x,y+t)\|\,\frac{\,\mathrm dt}{\|t\|}\right\\|_{3/2} \le 2\log(R/\varepsilon)\\|F\\|_3\\|G\\|_3.$$
  - Explanation: Places all finite truncations on a common full-measure set, establishes endpoint continuity, and bounds their absolute integrals.
- **claim-line-maximal-domination** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-line-maximal)): On either space, the centered maximal operator satisfies $$\mu\{Mf>\rho\}\le\frac3\rho\\|f\\|_1, \qquad \\|Mf\\|_p^p\le\frac{6p\,2^{p-1}}{p-1}\\|f\\|_p^p \quad(1<p<\infty).$$ Let $g_\sigma(t)=(2\pi\sigma)^{-1/2}e^{-t^2/(2\sigma)}$. There is an absolute constant $C$ such that $$\begin{aligned} \sum_m h g_\sigma((m-j)h)\|f(m)\| &\le C M_{\mathbb Z}f(j), &&\sigma\ge h^2, \label{eq:discrete-gaussian-maximal}\\ \int g_\sigma(t)\|f(x-t)\|\,\mathrm dt &\le C M_{\mathbb R}f(x), &&\sigma>0. \label{eq:continuous-gaussian-maximal} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Provides continuous and discrete centered maximal estimates and domination of Gaussian averages, with the discrete estimate restricted by the mesh scale.
- **claim-supported-energy-hessian** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hessian)): On the cone of matrices positive definite on a fixed support, $\Phi$ is smooth and $$d\Phi_P(K)=\frac32\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\sqrt P K),\qquad d^2\Phi_P(K,L)=\mathcal B_P(K,L).$$ In an orthonormal eigenbasis for the positive eigenvalues $p_i$ of $P$, $$\label{eq:hessian-metric} b_P(K)=\frac32\sum_{i,j} \frac{\|K_{ij}\|^2}{\sqrt{p_i}+\sqrt{p_j}}.$$ In particular, $\mathcal B_P$ is a positive definite symmetric bilinear form on the real space of supported Hermitian directions, and $$\|\mathcal B_P(K,L)\|\le b_P(K)^{1/2}b_P(L)^{1/2}.$$
  - Explanation: Identifies the derivatives of the matrix energy on a fixed support and its positive quadratic metric.
- **claim-hessian-order-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-order)): If $P,Q\ge0$ on the same space, $a\ge1$, and $P\le aQ$, then every Hermitian $K$ supported on $P$ is supported on $Q$, and $$\label{eq:matrix-order} b_P(K)\ge a^{-1/2}b_Q(K).$$
  - Explanation: Transfers a supported direction to the larger support and compares its quadratic costs under positive matrix order.
- **claim-inserted-gram-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-insertion)): For a linear map $R:E\to F$ and a Hermitian map $L:F\to F$, the matrix $R^*LR$ is supported on $R^*R$, and $$\label{eq:insertion-bound} b_{R^*R}(R^*LR) \le\frac34\lVert L\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^2\Phi(R^*R).$$
  - Explanation: Bounds an inserted Gram direction by the matrix energy and the squared operator norm of the Hermitian insertion.
- **claim-probability-average-convexity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-joint-convexity)): Let $(\Omega,\nu)$ be a probability space and $E$ a fixed subspace of a finite-dimensional complex Hilbert space. Suppose $T_\omega\ge0$ and $K_\omega=K_\omega^*$ are measurable matrices, $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}T_\omega=E$ and $K_\omega$ is supported on $T_\omega$ almost everywhere, and $$\int_\Omega\bigl(\lVert T_\omega\rVert_{\mathrm{op}} +\lVert K_\omega\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\bigr)\,\,\mathrm d\nu(\omega) <\infty.$$ Then $\overline T=\int T_\omega\,\,\mathrm d\nu$ has support $E$, $\overline K=\int K_\omega\,\,\mathrm d\nu$ is supported on $\overline T$, and $$\label{eq:joint-convexity} b_{\overline T}(\overline K) \le\int_\Omega b_{T_\omega}(K_\omega)\,\,\mathrm d\nu(\omega).$$ The right side may be infinite.
  - Explanation: Averaging Grams and supported directions does not increase the quadratic cost beyond the average cost; the common support and integrability assumptions are essential to the stated formulation.
- **claim-rank-change-gradient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rank-change)): For rectangular complex matrices $R_-,R_+$ of the same size, set $R(z)=(1-z)R_-+zR_+$ and $D=R_+-R_-$. Then $$\label{eq:rank-change-derivative} \Phi(R_+^*R_+)-\Phi(R_-^*R_-) =3\int_0^1\operatorname{Re} \langle R(z)\sqrt{R(z)^*R(z)},D\rangle_{\mathrm{HS}}\,\,\mathrm dz.$$ For every rectangular matrix $R$ and every entry $(i,j)$, $$\label{eq:entry-gradient} \|(R\sqrt{R^*R})_{ij}\| \le\lVert\operatorname{row}_iR\rVert_2 \lVert\operatorname{col}_jR\rVert_2.$$
  - Explanation: Expresses energy changes along an affine matrix path even when rank changes, and bounds each gradient entry by row and column norms.
- **claim-dimension-free-cyclic-trace** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-mixed-trace)): For Hermitian maps $D_v:H_v\to H_v$, put $D_* =\max_v\lVert D_v\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}$. Then $$\label{eq:mixed-trace} \left\|\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(D_0W_0D_1W_1D_2W_2)\right\| \le\frac53D_*\sum_{v=0}^2b_{T_v}(R_v^*D_vR_v).$$ The constant is independent of the dimensions and no Gram is assumed invertible.
  - Explanation: Controls the cyclic trace by supported star costs without requiring invertible Grams, uniformly in the dimensions.
- **claim-gaussian-heat-identities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-heat-identities)): For fixed finite arrays and arbitrary positive rates, the energies $e_v$ are smooth in the centers and positive variances. For $s>0$, $$\begin{aligned} 2\partial_s e_v &=\sum_i\alpha_i\partial_{p_i}^2e_v -\alpha_vV_v-\sum_{w\ne v}\alpha_wY_{v,w}, \label{eq:heat-identity}\\ \partial_{p_{v+1}}\partial_{p_{v-1}}e_v&=Z_v. \label{eq:heat-mixed} \end{aligned}$$ The plane integrals are finite, continuously differentiable in $s$, and twice continuously differentiable in $d$, with $$\label{eq:heat-plane-identities} \begin{aligned} 2\partial_sJ_v(d,s) &=\Sigma_\alpha\partial_d^2J_v(d,s) -\int_{\Pi_d}\left(\alpha_vV_v+ \sum_{w\ne v}\alpha_wY_{v,w}\right)\,\mathrm d\pi_d,\\ \partial_d^2J_v(d,s)&=\int_{\Pi_d}Z_v\,\,\mathrm d\pi_d. \end{aligned}$$ On compact positive scale intervals, the plane integrals of the absolute values of all derivatives used here grow at most polynomially in $\|d\|$.
  - Explanation: Relates scale derivatives and center derivatives of weighted matrix energies to their supported Hessian costs, including justified plane integration.
- **claim-plane-energy-dissipation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-plane-dissipation)): For fixed finite arrays and arbitrary positive rates, $$\label{eq:heat-plane-dissipation} 2\partial_sJ(d,s)\le\int_{\Pi_d}\sum_w \left[-\alpha_w+ \sqrt2\max\{0,\Sigma_\alpha/2-\alpha_w\}\right]V_w\,\,\mathrm d\pi_d.$$ In particular, put $\lambda=11/10$. For each permutation of $(1,\lambda,\lambda)$, $$\label{eq:heat-strict} \begin{gathered} -\partial_sJ(d,s)\ge c_*\int_{\Pi_d}\sum_wV_w\,\,\mathrm d\pi_d,\\ c_* =\tfrac12\min\{1-\tfrac35\sqrt2, \tfrac{11}{10}-\tfrac12\sqrt2\}>0. \end{gathered}$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the scale derivative of the summed plane energy and obtains strict dissipation for the specified comparable variance rates.
- **claim-mesh-uniform-initial-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-initial-energy)): For all $d\in\mathbb R$ and all variances $\sigma_v\ge h^2$, $$\label{eq:initial-energy} \begin{gathered} \int_{\Pi_d}\\|W_v\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^3\,\,\mathrm d\pi_d\le M n_v^3, \qquad M=1+\sqrt{2/\pi},\\ \int_{\Pi_d}\sum_ve_v\,\,\mathrm d\pi_d \le2\sqrt2 M\sum_v n_v^3. \end{gathered}$$ These bounds also hold with the original right-hand sides if any entries are replaced by entries of smaller absolute value.
  - Explanation: Bounds integrated edge and star energies by the original discrete input norms whenever the Gaussian variances dominate the squared mesh.
- **claim-single-edge-dissipation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-single-edge-energy)): Fix $v\ne w$, retain the weighted block $C=C_{v,w}$, and delete the other block of the star at $v$. For arbitrary positive rates, set $$\begin{aligned} e^e_{v,w}&=\Phi(CC^*),\qquad V^e_{v,w}=b_{C^*C}(C^*N_vC),\qquad Y^e_{v,w}=b_{CC^*}(CN_wC^*),\\ J^e_{v,w}(s)&=\iint_{\mathbb R^2}e^e_{v,w}\,\,\mathrm dp_v\,\,\mathrm dp_w. \end{aligned}$$ The plane integrals of $e^e_{v,w}$, $V^e_{v,w}$, and $Y^e_{v,w}$ are independent of $d$. Moreover, $$\label{eq:heat-single-edge} -2\partial_sJ^e_{v,w}(s) =\iint_{\mathbb R^2} (\alpha_vV^e_{v,w}+\alpha_wY^e_{v,w})\,\,\mathrm dp_v\,\,\mathrm dp_w.$$ If $n_e$ denotes the discrete $L^3$ norm of this edge’s unweighted array, then, whenever $\alpha_vs_0,\alpha_ws_0\ge h^2$ and $s_1>s_0$, $$\label{eq:heat-single-edge-integral} \int_{s_0}^{s_1}\iint_{\mathbb R^2} (\alpha_vV^e_{v,w}+\alpha_wY^e_{v,w}) \,\,\mathrm dp_v\,\,\mathrm dp_w\,\,\mathrm ds \le2M n_e^3.$$ For the original two-block star, $Y_{v,w}\le Y^e_{v,w}$.
  - Explanation: An isolated edge has exact integrated dissipation for arbitrary positive rates, supplying control when summed star dissipation is unavailable.
- **claim-smooth-annular-switches** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-smooth-switches)): Let $G_v\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^2)$ be complex. At each output pair $(a,b)$, make a step choice of at most $n\ge1$ disjoint radius intervals $(\varepsilon_j,R_j)$ and coefficients $\|\beta_j\|\le1$. For $$\mathcal K_{a,b}(t)=\sum_j\beta_j I_{\varepsilon_j,R_j}(t)$$ one has $$\|\Lambda_{\mathcal K}(G_0,G_1,G_2)\| \le C\sqrt n\prod_{v=0}^2\\|G_v\\|_3.$$ The constant is independent of the step partition, the menu of choices, and all endpoints.
  - Explanation: Controls smooth annular linearizations for step choices with disjoint intervals and bounded complex coefficients, uniformly in all endpoints and the partition.
- **claim-repeated-mask-dissipation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-repeated-dissipation)): Use the masked arrays above and variances $\sigma_v=\alpha_vs$, where $\boldsymbol\alpha$ is any permutation of $(1,11/10,11/10)$. With $n_v$ denoting the norms of the original arrays, $$\label{eq:repeated-dissipation} \int_{s_-}^{s_+}\int_{\Pi_0}\sum_vV_v\,\mathrm d\pi_0\,\mathrm ds \le C\left(\sum_vn_v^3+n\bigl(n_0^3+n_0^2(n_1+n_2)\bigr)\right).$$
  - Explanation: Adds the entrywise costs of switching the dual array to the fixed-array dissipation bound; the mixed switch terms retain quadratic dependence on the dual norm.
- **claim-uniform-frequency-block** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-frequency-block)): Let $\mathcal I\subset\mathbb Z$ be finite and nonempty, and set $L_k=2^k$. Let $A_0,A_1,A_2$ be finite arrays on the lattice $u_i=ih$, with the norms $n_v$ defined in the heat-flow preliminaries. Suppose that measurable coefficients $\mu_{ij,k}:\Xi_D\to\mathbb C$ satisfy $$\label{eq:frequency-coefficient-condition} \|\mu_{ij,k}(\xi)\|\leq C_1, \qquad \sum_{k\in\mathcal I}\int_{\Xi_D} \|\mu_{ij,k}(\xi)\|^2\,\,\mathrm d\gamma(\xi)\leq C_1^2$$ for every output index pair $(i,j)$, with the first inequality holding almost everywhere. If $$\label{eq:frequency-mesh} h^2\leq\frac{q\min_{k\in\mathcal I}L_k^2}{16D^2},$$ then $$\label{eq:frequency-finite-estimate} \left\|h^3\int_{\Xi_D}\sum_{k\in\mathcal I}\sum_{i,j,l} A_0(i,j)\mu_{ij,k}(\xi)A_1(j,l)A_2(l,i) \mathop{\mathrm{Dil}}_{L_k}H_\xi(u_i+u_j+u_l)\,\,\mathrm d\gamma(\xi)\right\| \leq C(q,C_1)\prod_{v=0}^2n_v.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds a finite modulated Gaussian block under pointwise coefficient bounds, an averaged square-sum condition, and the prescribed mesh restriction, uniformly in frequency size.
- **claim-uniform-mixed-boundary** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-frequency-boundary)): Fix distinct vertices $v,w\in\{0,1\}$, so that $\{v,w\}=\{0,1\}$. Let the edge joining $v,w$ have array $B$, indexed in the original endpoint order $(0,1)$, and let the edge joining $v,2$ have array $E$, indexed in the order $(v,2)$. Use variances $(\sigma_v,\sigma_w,\sigma_2)=(\theta s,s,s)$, where $0<\theta\leq1/16$ and $h^2\leq\theta s$. Write $\,\mathrm d\nu_s(d)=g_{(1-\theta)s}(d)\,\,\mathrm dd$. There is a nonnegative array $\mathcal H_E(i,j)$, independent of $s,\theta$, such that $$\begin{aligned} &\int_{\mathbb R}\int_{\Pi_d} \\|C_{v,w}\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\\|C_{v,2}\\|_{\mathrm{HS}} \,\,\mathrm d\pi_d\,\,\mathrm d\nu_s(d) \leq C\sum_{i,j}h^2\|B(i,j)\|^2\mathcal H_E(i,j), \label{eq:frequency-boundary-mixed}\\ &\left(\sum_{i,j}h^2\mathcal H_E(i,j)^3\right)^{1/3} \leq C\left(\sum_{m,l}h^2\|E(m,l)\|^3\right)^{1/3}. \label{eq:frequency-boundary-norm} \end{aligned}$$ Here $(i,j)$ are always in the original order of the endpoints of edge $0$; $i_v,i_w$ denote the same two indices in the order $(v,w)$. The input arrays $B,E$ are extended by zero to $\mathbb Z^2$.
  - Explanation: Constructs a scale-independent maximal majorant for the mixed boundary energy, avoiding a loss from the narrowed Gaussian variance.
- **claim-continuous-frequency-block** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-frequency-continuum)): Let $q,D,\Xi_D,\mathcal I$ be as in Proposition <a href="#prop:frequency-block" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:frequency-block">17</a>. Suppose that $\mu_{a,b,k}(\xi)$ is constant in $(a,b)$ on every cell of one finite rectangular partition, independent of $\xi$ and common to all $k$. Suppose its values on these cells are bounded measurable functions of $\xi$, and that $$\label{eq:frequency-continuous-condition} \|\mu_{a,b,k}(\xi)\|\leq C_1, \qquad \sum_k\int_{\Xi_D}\|\mu_{a,b,k}(\xi)\|^2\,\,\mathrm d\gamma(\xi) \leq C_1^2.$$ Define $$\label{eq:frequency-continuous-kernel} \mathcal K_{a,b}(t) =\int_{\Xi_D}\sum_{k\in\mathcal I} \mu_{a,b,k}(\xi)\mathop{\mathrm{Dil}}_{L_k}H_\xi(t)\,\,\mathrm d\gamma(\xi).$$ Then, for complex $G_v\in L^3(\mathbb R^2)$, $$\label{eq:frequency-continuous-estimate} \|\Lambda_{\mathcal K}(G_0,G_1,G_2)\| \leq C(q,C_1)\prod_{v=0}^2\\|G_v\\|_3.$$
  - Explanation: Transfers the finite frequency-block bound to continuous triangular forms with a common rectangular step partition and then to general complex inputs.
- **claim-gaussian-kernel-envelope** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-gaussian-envelope)): Suppose that $c>0$ and that a measurable kernel $f$ satisfies $\|f(z)\|\le e^{-cz^2}w(z)$, where $w\ge0$ and $\\|w\\|_5\le\delta$. Then, for every $L>0$, $$\int_{\mathbb R}\|F(x+t,y)G(x,y+t)\|\,\|\mathop{\mathrm{Dil}}_L f(t)\|\,\,\mathrm dt \le C_c\delta\,\mathcal M_{F,G}(x,y) \label{eq:gaussian-envelope}$$ at every point where the right side is finite. In particular, a bound $\|f(z)\|\le A e^{-cz^2}$ gives <a href="#eq:gaussian-envelope" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:gaussian-envelope">[eq:gaussian-envelope]</a> with $C_c\delta$ replaced by $C_cA$.
  - Explanation: Dominates dilated Gaussian-decaying kernels by directional maximal averages of the inputs, uniformly in the dilation.
- **claim-controlled-rough-kernel-family** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-rough-family)): Fix $A\ge1$ and $c>0$. Let $n\ge1$, and let $\mathcal I\subset\mathbb Z$ be finite. For $k\in\mathcal I$, let $K_{a,b,k}\colon\mathbb R\to\mathbb C$ and $m_{a,b,k}\in\{0,1,2,\ldots\}$ be finite-valued step choices in $(a,b)$, with $K_{a,b,k}=0$ whenever $m_{a,b,k}=0$. When $m_{a,b,k}\ge1$, suppose that $K_{a,b,k}$ is $C^1$ outside a set of at most $A m_{a,b,k}$ points and that, outside that set, $$\|K_{a,b,k}(z)\|+\|K_{a,b,k}'(z)\|\le A e^{-cz^2}. \label{eq:rough-family-decay}$$ Assume also that $$\int_{\mathbb R}z^\ell K_{a,b,k}(z)\,\,\mathrm dz=0 \quad(\ell=0,1,2), \qquad \sum_{k\in\mathcal I}m_{a,b,k}\le An, \quad m_{a,b,k}\le A\sqrt n. \label{eq:rough-family-hypotheses}$$ For $$\mathcal K_{a,b}(t) =\sum_{k\in\mathcal I}\mathop{\mathrm{Dil}}_{2^k}K_{a,b,k}(t),$$ and smooth compactly supported $G_0,G_1,G_2$, one has $$\|\Lambda_{\mathcal K}(G_0,G_1,G_2)\| \le C_{A,c}\sqrt n\log(2+n)\prod_{v=0}^2\\|G_v\\|_3. \label{eq:rough-family-bound}$$
  - Explanation: Combines Gaussian decay, controlled jump counts, and vanishing moments to bound a finite rough family through frequency blocks and a summable cutoff error.
- **claim-odd-rough-kernel-family** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-odd-family)): Proposition <a href="#prop:rough-family" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:rough-family">21</a> remains valid if the three moment conditions in <a href="#eq:rough-family-hypotheses" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rough-family-hypotheses">[eq:rough-family-hypotheses]</a> are replaced by the assumption that every $K_{a,b,k}$ is odd almost everywhere.
  - Explanation: Replaces the moment cancellation requirement by oddness while retaining the other rough-family hypotheses, using a summable dilation identity to remove the first moment.
- **claim-grouped-endpoint-errors** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-endpoint-errors)): At each $(a,b)$, choose at most $n$ pairwise disjoint open radius intervals $(\varepsilon_j,R_j)$ with $0<\varepsilon_j<R_j<\infty$, and complex coefficients $\beta_j$ with $\|\beta_j\|\le1$. Suppose that these choices form a finite-valued step function of $(a,b)$. Then the kernel $$\mathcal E_{a,b}(t) =\sum_j\beta_j \bigl(E_{\varepsilon_j}(t)-E_{R_j}(t)\bigr)$$ satisfies $$\|\Lambda_{\mathcal E}(G_0,G_1,G_2)\| \le C\sqrt n\log(2+n)\prod_{v=0}^2\\|G_v\\|_3 \label{eq:endpoint-error-bound}$$ for smooth compactly supported inputs.
  - Explanation: Controls all hard-to-smooth endpoint errors together by grouping comparable radii and exploiting disjointness within each group.
- **claim-hard-maximal-continuity** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-maximal)): For complex $F,G\in L^3(\mathbb R^2)$, define $$B_*(F,G)(x,y) =\sup_{0<\varepsilon<R<\infty}\|B_{\varepsilon,R}(F,G)(x,y)\|$$ on the common full-measure set of Lemma <a href="#lem:finite-truncations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:finite-truncations">3</a>, and set it to zero elsewhere. This function is measurable, and there is an absolute constant $C_M$ such that $$\label{eq:hard-maximal-bound} \\|B_*(F,G)\\|_p\le C_M\\|F\\|_3\\|G\\|_3.$$ The supremum includes both finite hard truncation endpoints. Moreover, $B_*$ is a continuous map from $L^3(\mathbb R^2)\times L^3(\mathbb R^2)$ to $L^p(\mathbb R^2)$ and is the unique continuous extension of its values on compactly supported smooth pairs.
  - Explanation: Bounds the hard maximal operator over both endpoints and establishes continuity and uniqueness of its extension from smooth pairs.
- **claim-joint-bilinear-principal-value** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-bilinear-pv)): For every complex $F,G\in L^3(\mathbb R^2)$, the joint limit $$B(F,G)=\lim_{\substack{\varepsilon\downarrow0\\R\uparrow\infty}} B_{\varepsilon,R}(F,G)$$ exists almost everywhere and in $L^p(\mathbb R^2)$, and $$\\|B(F,G)\\|_p\le C_M\\|F\\|_3\\|G\\|_3.$$ More precisely, the entire tail converges in the maximal sense $$\label{eq:maximal-tail-convergence} \left\\|\sup_{\substack{0<\varepsilon<1/n\\R>n}} \|B_{\varepsilon,R}(F,G)-B(F,G)\|\right\\|_p \longrightarrow0.$$ The operator $B$ is the unique bounded complex bilinear extension of the principal-value operator on compactly supported smooth inputs.
  - Explanation: Obtains joint almost-everywhere and norm principal values, including convergence of the entire maximal tail, and identifies the unique bounded bilinear extension.
- **claim-flat-scalar-principal-value** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-scalar-pv)): For every complex $L^3$ triple, each finite flat truncation is absolutely integrable, and $$\sup_{0<\varepsilon<R<\infty} \|\mathcal L_{\varepsilon,R}(F_0,F_1,F_2)\| \le C_M\prod_{v=0}^2\\|F_v\\|_3.$$ Its joint scalar principal value exists and equals $\int_{\mathbb R^2}F_0 B(F_1,F_2)$. This limiting form is the unique bounded complex trilinear extension of its compactly supported smooth definition.
  - Explanation: Pairs the bilinear transform with the remaining input to obtain uniformly bounded flat scalar truncations and a unique trilinear principal value.
- **claim-flat-simplex-equivalence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-flat-equivalence)): For every complex $G_0,G_1,G_2\in L^3(\mathbb R^2)$, each finite simplex truncation is absolutely integrable, and $$\sup_{0<\varepsilon<R<\infty} \|\Lambda_{\varepsilon,R}(G_0,G_1,G_2)\| \le C_M\prod_{v=0}^2\\|G_v\\|_3.$$ Its joint scalar principal value exists and is the unique bounded complex trilinear extension of its compactly supported smooth definition. The optimal constants in the uniform finite flat and simplex scalar estimates are equal.
  - Explanation: Transfers absolute integrability, uniform bounds, and joint scalar convergence between flat and simplex coordinates with equality of their optimal scalar constants.

          "exact_quote": "On either space, the centered maximal operator satisfies $$\\mu\\{Mf>\\rho\\}\\le\\frac3\\rho\\|f\\|_1,\n \\qquad\n \\|Mf\\|_p^p\\le\\frac{6p\\,2^{p-1}}{p-1}\\|f\\|_p^p\n \\quad(1<p<\\infty).$$ Let $g_\\sigma(t)=(2\\pi\\sigma)^{-1/2}e^{-t^2/(2\\sigma)}$. There is an absolute constant $C$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sum_m h g_\\sigma((m-j)h)|f(m)|\n   &\\le C M_{\\mathbb Z}f(j), &&\\sigma\\ge h^2,\n   \\label{eq:discrete-gaussian-maximal}\\\\\n \\int g_\\sigma(t)|f(x-t)|\\,\\mathrm dt\n   &\\le C M_{\\mathbb R}f(x), &&\\sigma>0.\n   \\label{eq:continuous-gaussian-maximal}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-082.1"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-hessian",
        "exact_quote": "On the cone of matrices positive definite on a fixed support, $\\Phi$ is smooth and $$d\\Phi_P(K)=\\frac32\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}(\\sqrt P K),\\qquad\n d^2\\Phi_P(K,L)=\\mathcal B_P(K,L).$$ In an orthonormal eigenbasis for the positive eigenvalues $p_i$ of $P$, $$\\label{eq:hessian-metric}\n b_P(K)=\\frac32\\sum_{i,j}\n \\frac{|K_{ij}|^2}{\\sqrt{p_i}+\\sqrt{p_j}}.$$ In particular, $\\mathcal B_P$ is a positive definite symmetric bilinear form on the real space of supported Hermitian directions, and $$|\\mathcal B_P(K,L)|\\le b_P(K)^{1/2}b_P(L)^{1/2}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-082.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-hessian",
          "exact_quote": "On the cone of matrices positive definite on a fixed support, $\\Phi$ is smooth and $$d\\Phi_P(K)=\\frac32\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}(\\sqrt P K),\\qquad\n d^2\\Phi_P(K,L)=\\mathcal B_P(K,L).$$ In an orthonormal eigenbasis for the positive eigenvalues $p_i$ of $P$, $$\\label{eq:hessian-metric}\n b_P(K)=\\frac32\\sum_{i,j}\n \\frac{|K_{ij}|^2}{\\sqrt{p_i}+\\sqrt{p_j}}.$$ In particular, $\\mathcal B_P$ is a positive definite symmetric bilinear form on the real space of supported Hermitian directions, and $$|\\mathcal B_P(K,L)|\\le b_P(K)^{1/2}b_P(L)^{1/2}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-082.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "On the cone of matrices positive definite on a fixed support, $\\Phi$ is smooth and $$d\\Phi_P(K)=\\frac32\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}(\\sqrt P K),\\qquad\n d^2\\Phi_P(K,L)=\\mathcal B_P(K,L).$$ In an orthonormal eigenbasis for the positive eigenvalues $p_i$ of $P$, $$\\label{eq:hessian-metric}\n b_P(K)=\\frac32\\sum_{i,j}\n \\frac{|K_{ij}|^2}{\\sqrt{p_i}+\\sqrt{p_j}}.$$ In particular, $\\mathcal B_P$ is a positive definite symmetric bilinear form on the real space of supported Hermitian directions, and $$|\\mathcal B_P(K,L)|\\le b_P(K)^{1/2}b_P(L)^{1/2}.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-joint-convexity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(\\Omega,\\nu)$ be a probability space and $E$ a fixed subspace of a finite-dimensional complex Hilbert space. Suppose $T_\\omega\\ge0$ and $K_\\omega=K_\\omega^*$ are measurable matrices, $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}T_\\omega=E$ and $K_\\omega$ is supported on $T_\\omega$ almost everywhere, and $$\\int_\\Omega\\bigl(\\lVert T_\\omega\\rVert_{\\mathrm{op}}\n                  +\\lVert K_\\omega\\rVert_{\\mathrm{op}}\\bigr)\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\nu(\\omega)\n <\\infty.$$ Then $\\overline T=\\int T_\\omega\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\nu$ has support $E$, $\\overline K=\\int K_\\omega\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\nu$ is supported on $\\overline T$, and $$\\label{eq:joint-convexity}\n b_{\\overline T}(\\overline K)\n \\le\\int_\\Omega b_{T_\\omega}(K_\\omega)\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\nu(\\omega).$$ The right side may be infinite.",
        "source_id": "PM-082.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-joint-convexity",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(\\Omega,\\nu)$ be a probability space and $E$ a fixed subspace of a finite-dimensional complex Hilbert space. Suppose $T_\\omega\\ge0$ and $K_\\omega=K_\\omega^*$ are measurable matrices, $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}T_\\omega=E$ and $K_\\omega$ is supported on $T_\\omega$ almost everywhere, and $$\\int_\\Omega\\bigl(\\lVert T_\\omega\\rVert_{\\mathrm{op}}\n                  +\\lVert K_\\omega\\rVert_{\\mathrm{op}}\\bigr)\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\nu(\\omega)\n <\\infty.$$ Then $\\overline T=\\int T_\\omega\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\nu$ has support $E$, $\\overline K=\\int K_\\omega\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\nu$ is supported on $\\overline T$, and $$\\label{eq:joint-convexity}\n b_{\\overline T}(\\overline K)\n \\le\\int_\\Omega b_{T_\\omega}(K_\\omega)\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\nu(\\omega).$$ The right side may be infinite.",
          "source_id": "PM-082.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $(\\Omega,\\nu)$ be a probability space and $E$ a fixed subspace of a finite-dimensional complex Hilbert space. Suppose $T_\\omega\\ge0$ and $K_\\omega=K_\\omega^*$ are measurable matrices, $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}T_\\omega=E$ and $K_\\omega$ is supported on $T_\\omega$ almost everywhere, and $$\\int_\\Omega\\bigl(\\lVert T_\\omega\\rVert_{\\mathrm{op}}\n                  +\\lVert K_\\omega\\rVert_{\\mathrm{op}}\\bigr)\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\nu(\\omega)\n <\\infty.$$ Then $\\overline T=\\int T_\\omega\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\nu$ has support $E$, $\\overline K=\\int K_\\omega\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\nu$ is supported on $\\overline T$, and $$\\label{eq:joint-convexity}\n b_{\\overline T}(\\overline K)\n \\le\\int_\\Omega b_{T_\\omega}(K_\\omega)\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\nu(\\omega).$$ The right side may be infinite.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rank-change",
        "exact_quote": "For rectangular complex matrices $R_-,R_+$ of the same size, set $R(z)=(1-z)R_-+zR_+$ and $D=R_+-R_-$. Then $$\\label{eq:rank-change-derivative}\n \\Phi(R_+^*R_+)-\\Phi(R_-^*R_-)\n =3\\int_0^1\\operatorname{Re}\n \\langle R(z)\\sqrt{R(z)^*R(z)},D\\rangle_{\\mathrm{HS}}\\,\\,\\mathrm dz.$$ For every rectangular matrix $R$ and every entry $(i,j)$, $$\\label{eq:entry-gradient}\n |(R\\sqrt{R^*R})_{ij}|\n \\le\\lVert\\operatorname{row}_iR\\rVert_2\n       \\lVert\\operatorname{col}_jR\\rVert_2.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-082.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-rank-change",
          "exact_quote": "For rectangular complex matrices $R_-,R_+$ of the same size, set $R(z)=(1-z)R_-+zR_+$ and $D=R_+-R_-$. Then $$\\label{eq:rank-change-derivative}\n \\Phi(R_+^*R_+)-\\Phi(R_-^*R_-)\n =3\\int_0^1\\operatorname{Re}\n \\langle R(z)\\sqrt{R(z)^*R(z)},D\\rangle_{\\mathrm{HS}}\\,\\,\\mathrm dz.$$ For every rectangular matrix $R$ and every entry $(i,j)$, $$\\label{eq:entry-gradient}\n |(R\\sqrt{R^*R})_{ij}|\n \\le\\lVert\\operatorname{row}_iR\\rVert_2\n       \\lVert\\operatorname{col}_jR\\rVert_2.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-082.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For rectangular complex matrices $R_-,R_+$ of the same size, set $R(z)=(1-z)R_-+zR_+$ and $D=R_+-R_-$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-heat-identities",
        "exact_quote": "For fixed finite arrays and arbitrary positive rates, the energies $e_v$ are smooth in the centers and positive variances. For $s>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n 2\\partial_s e_v\n &=\\sum_i\\alpha_i\\partial_{p_i}^2e_v\n   -\\alpha_vV_v-\\sum_{w\\ne v}\\alpha_wY_{v,w},\n   \\label{eq:heat-identity}\\\\\n \\partial_{p_{v+1}}\\partial_{p_{v-1}}e_v&=Z_v.\n   \\label{eq:heat-mixed}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The plane integrals are finite, continuously differentiable in $s$, and twice continuously differentiable in $d$, with $$\\label{eq:heat-plane-identities}\n \\begin{aligned}\n 2\\partial_sJ_v(d,s)\n &=\\Sigma_\\alpha\\partial_d^2J_v(d,s)\n   -\\int_{\\Pi_d}\\left(\\alpha_vV_v+\n                  \\sum_{w\\ne v}\\alpha_wY_{v,w}\\right)\\,\\mathrm d\\pi_d,\\\\\n \\partial_d^2J_v(d,s)&=\\int_{\\Pi_d}Z_v\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\pi_d.\n \\end{aligned}$$ On compact positive scale intervals, the plane integrals of the absolute values of all derivatives used here grow at most polynomially in $|d|$.",
        "source_id": "PM-082.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-heat-identities",
          "exact_quote": "For fixed finite arrays and arbitrary positive rates, the energies $e_v$ are smooth in the centers and positive variances.           "source_id": "PM-082.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For fixed finite arrays and arbitrary positive rates, the energies $e_v$ are smooth in the centers and positive variances. For $s>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n 2\\partial_s e_v\n &=\\sum_i\\alpha_i\\partial_{p_i}^2e_v\n   -\\alpha_vV_v-\\sum_{w\\ne v}\\alpha_wY_{v,w},\n   \\label{eq:heat-identity}\\\\\n \\partial_{p_{v+1}}\\partial_{p_{v-1}}e_v&=Z_v.\n   \\label{eq:heat-mixed}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The plane integrals are finite, continuously differentiable in $s$, and twice continuously differentiable in $d$, with $$\\label{eq:heat-plane-identities}\n \\begin{aligned}\n 2\\partial_sJ_v(d,s)\n &=\\Sigma_\\alpha\\partial_d^2J_v(d,s)\n   -\\int_{\\Pi_d}\\left(\\alpha_vV_v+\n                  \\sum_{w\\ne v}\\alpha_wY_{v,w}\\right)\\,\\mathrm d\\pi_d,\\\\\n \\partial_d^2J_v(d,s)&=\\int_{\\Pi_d}Z_v\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\pi_d.\n \\end{aligned}$$ On compact positive scale intervals, the plane integrals of the absolute values of all derivatives used here grow at most polynomially in $|d|$.",
      "explanation": "Relates scale derivatives and center derivatives of weighted matrix energies to their supported Hessian costs, including justified plane integration.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-plane-dissipation",
        "exact_quote": "For fixed finite arrays and arbitrary positive rates, $$\\label{eq:heat-plane-dissipation}\n 2\\partial_sJ(d,s)\\le\\int_{\\Pi_d}\\sum_w\n \\left[-\\alpha_w+\n \\sqrt2\\max\\{0,\\Sigma_\\alpha/2-\\alpha_w\\}\\right]V_w\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\pi_d.$$ In particular, put $\\lambda=11/10$. For each permutation of $(1,\\lambda,\\lambda)$, $$\\label{eq:heat-strict}\n \\begin{gathered}\n -\\partial_sJ(d,s)\\ge c_*\\int_{\\Pi_d}\\sum_wV_w\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\pi_d,\\\\\n c_* =\\tfrac12\\min\\{1-\\tfrac35\\sqrt2,\n                      \\tfrac{11}{10}-\\tfrac12\\sqrt2\\}>0.\n \\end{gathered}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-082.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-plane-dissipation",

For each permutation of $(1,\\lambda,\\lambda)$, $$\\label{eq:heat-strict}\n \\begin{gathered}\n -\\partial_sJ(d,s)\\ge c_*\\int_{\\Pi_d}\\sum_wV_w\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\pi_d,\\\\\n c_* =\\tfrac12\\min\\{1-\\tfrac35\\sqrt2,\n                      \\tfrac{11}{10}-\\tfrac12\\sqrt2\\}>0.\n \\end{gathered}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-082.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For fixed finite arrays and arbitrary positive rates, $$\\label{eq:heat-plane-dissipation}\n 2\\partial_sJ(d,s)\\le\\int_{\\Pi_d}\\sum_w\n \\left[-\\alpha_w+\n \\sqrt2\\max\\{0,\\Sigma_\\alpha/2-\\alpha_w\\}\\right]V_w\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\pi_d.$$ In particular, put $\\lambda=11/10$. For each permutation of $(1,\\lambda,\\lambda)$, $$\\label{eq:heat-strict}\n \\begin{gathered}\n -\\partial_sJ(d,s)\\ge c_*\\int_{\\Pi_d}\\sum_wV_w\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\pi_d,\\\\\n c_* =\\tfrac12\\min\\{1-\\tfrac35\\sqrt2,\n                      \\tfrac{11}{10}-\\tfrac12\\sqrt2\\}>0.\n \\end{gathered}$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-single-edge-energy",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $v\\ne w$, retain the weighted block $C=C_{v,w}$, and delete the other block of the star at $v$. For arbitrary positive rates, set $$\\begin{aligned}\n e^e_{v,w}&=\\Phi(CC^*),\\qquad\n V^e_{v,w}=b_{C^*C}(C^*N_vC),\\qquad\n Y^e_{v,w}=b_{CC^*}(CN_wC^*),\\\\\n J^e_{v,w}(s)&=\\iint_{\\mathbb R^2}e^e_{v,w}\\,\\,\\mathrm dp_v\\,\\,\\mathrm dp_w.\n \\end{aligned}$$ The plane integrals of $e^e_{v,w}$, $V^e_{v,w}$, and $Y^e_{v,w}$ are independent of $d$. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:heat-single-edge}\n -2\\partial_sJ^e_{v,w}(s)\n =\\iint_{\\mathbb R^2}\n      (\\alpha_vV^e_{v,w}+\\alpha_wY^e_{v,w})\\,\\,\\mathrm dp_v\\,\\,\\mathrm dp_w.$$ If $n_e$ denotes the discrete $L^3$ norm of this edge’s unweighted array, then, whenever $\\alpha_vs_0,\\alpha_ws_0\\ge h^2$ and $s_1>s_0$, $$\\label{eq:heat-single-edge-integral}\n \\int_{s_0}^{s_1}\\iint_{\\mathbb R^2}\n      (\\alpha_vV^e_{v,w}+\\alpha_wY^e_{v,w})\n          \\,\\,\\mathrm dp_v\\,\\,\\mathrm dp_w\\,\\,\\mathrm ds\n \\le2M n_e^3.$$ For the original two-block star, $Y_{v,w}\\le Y^e_{v,w}$.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $G_v\\in C_c^\\infty(\\mathbb R^2)$ be complex.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-frequency-block",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal I\\subset\\mathbb Z$ be finite and nonempty, and set $L_k=2^k$. Let $A_0,A_1,A_2$ be finite arrays on the lattice $u_i=ih$, with the norms $n_v$ defined in the heat-flow preliminaries. Suppose that measurable coefficients $\\mu_{ij,k}:\\Xi_D\\to\\mathbb C$ satisfy $$\\label{eq:frequency-coefficient-condition}\n |\\mu_{ij,k}(\\xi)|\\leq C_1,\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{k\\in\\mathcal I}\\int_{\\Xi_D}\n           |\\mu_{ij,k}(\\xi)|^2\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\gamma(\\xi)\\leq C_1^2$$ for every output index pair $(i,j)$, with the first inequality holding almost everywhere. If $$\\label{eq:frequency-mesh}\n h^2\\leq\\frac{q\\min_{k\\in\\mathcal I}L_k^2}{16D^2},$$ then $$\\label{eq:frequency-finite-estimate}\n \\left|h^3\\int_{\\Xi_D}\\sum_{k\\in\\mathcal I}\\sum_{i,j,l}\n A_0(i,j)\\mu_{ij,k}(\\xi)A_1(j,l)A_2(l,i)\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Dil}}_{L_k}H_\\xi(u_i+u_j+u_l)\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\gamma(\\xi)\\right|\n \\leq C(q,C_1)\\prod_{v=0}^2n_v.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-082.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

If $$\\label{eq:frequency-mesh}\n h^2\\leq\\frac{q\\min_{k\\in\\mathcal I}L_k^2}{16D^2},$$ then $$\\label{eq:frequency-finite-estimate}\n \\left|h^3\\int_{\\Xi_D}\\sum_{k\\in\\mathcal I}\\sum_{i,j,l}\n A_0(i,j)\\mu_{ij,k}(\\xi)A_1(j,l)A_2(l,i)\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Dil}}_{L_k}H_\\xi(u_i+u_j+u_l)\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\gamma(\\xi)\\right|\n \\leq C(q,C_1)\\prod_{v=0}^2n_v.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-frequency-boundary",
        "exact_quote": "Fix distinct vertices $v,w\\in\\{0,1\\}$, so that $\\{v,w\\}=\\{0,1\\}$. Let the edge joining $v,w$ have array $B$, indexed in the original endpoint order $(0,1)$, and let the edge joining $v,2$ have array $E$, indexed in the order $(v,2)$. Use variances $(\\sigma_v,\\sigma_w,\\sigma_2)=(\\theta s,s,s)$, where $0<\\theta\\leq1/16$ and $h^2\\leq\\theta s$. Write $\\,\\mathrm d\\nu_s(d)=g_{(1-\\theta)s}(d)\\,\\,\\mathrm dd$. There is a nonnegative array $\\mathcal H_E(i,j)$, independent of $s,\\theta$, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\int_{\\mathbb R}\\int_{\\Pi_d}\n       \\|C_{v,w}\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\\|C_{v,2}\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}\n                  \\,\\,\\mathrm d\\pi_d\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\nu_s(d)\n \\leq C\\sum_{i,j}h^2|B(i,j)|^2\\mathcal H_E(i,j),\n \\label{eq:frequency-boundary-mixed}\\\\\n &\\left(\\sum_{i,j}h^2\\mathcal H_E(i,j)^3\\right)^{1/3}\n \\leq C\\left(\\sum_{m,l}h^2|E(m,l)|^3\\right)^{1/3}.\n \\label{eq:frequency-boundary-norm}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $(i,j)$ are always in the original order of the endpoints of edge $0$; $i_v,i_w$ denote the same two indices in the order $(v,w)$. The input arrays $B,E$ are extended by zero to $\\mathbb Z^2$.",
        "source_id": "PM-082.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-frequency-boundary",
          "exact_quote": "Fix distinct vertices $v,w\\in\\{0,1\\}$, so that $\\{v,w\\}=\\{0,1\\}$. Use variances $(\\sigma_v,\\sigma_w,\\sigma_2)=(\\theta s,s,s)$, where $0<\\theta\\leq1/16$ and $h^2\\leq\\theta s$. Write $\\,\\mathrm d\\nu_s(d)=g_{(1-\\theta)s}(d)\\,\\,\\mathrm dd$. There is a nonnegative array $\\mathcal H_E(i,j)$, independent of $s,\\theta$, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\int_{\\mathbb R}\\int_{\\Pi_d}\n       \\|C_{v,w}\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\\|C_{v,2}\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}\n                  \\,\\,\\mathrm d\\pi_d\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\nu_s(d)\n \\leq C\\sum_{i,j}h^2|B(i,j)|^2\\mathcal H_E(i,j),\n \\label{eq:frequency-boundary-mixed}\\\\\n &\\left(\\sum_{i,j}h^2\\mathcal H_E(i,j)^3\\right)^{1/3}\n \\leq C\\left(\\sum_{m,l}h^2|E(m,l)|^3\\right)^{1/3}.\n \\label{eq:frequency-boundary-norm}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $(i,j)$ are always in the original order of the endpoints of edge $0$; $i_v,i_w$ denote the same two indices in the order $(v,w)$. The input arrays $B,E$ are extended by zero to $\\mathbb Z^2$.",

      "exact_statement": "Fix distinct vertices $v,w\\in\\{0,1\\}$, so that $\\{v,w\\}=\\{0,1\\}$. Let the edge joining $v,w$ have array $B$, indexed in the original endpoint order $(0,1)$, and let the edge joining $v,2$ have array $E$, indexed in the order $(v,2)$. Use variances $(\\sigma_v,\\sigma_w,\\sigma_2)=(\\theta s,s,s)$, where $0<\\theta\\leq1/16$ and $h^2\\leq\\theta s$. Write $\\,\\mathrm d\\nu_s(d)=g_{(1-\\theta)s}(d)\\,\\,\\mathrm dd$. There is a nonnegative array $\\mathcal H_E(i,j)$, independent of $s,\\theta$, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\int_{\\mathbb R}\\int_{\\Pi_d}\n       \\|C_{v,w}\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\\|C_{v,2}\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}\n                  \\,\\,\\mathrm d\\pi_d\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\nu_s(d)\n \\leq C\\sum_{i,j}h^2|B(i,j)|^2\\mathcal H_E(i,j),\n \\label{eq:frequency-boundary-mixed}\\\\\n &\\left(\\sum_{i,j}h^2\\mathcal H_E(i,j)^3\\right)^{1/3}\n \\leq C\\left(\\sum_{m,l}h^2|E(m,l)|^3\\right)^{1/3}.\n \\label{eq:frequency-boundary-norm}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $(i,j)$ are always in the original order of the endpoints of edge $0$; $i_v,i_w$ denote the same two indices in the order $(v,w)$. The input arrays $B,E$ are extended by zero to $\\mathbb Z^2$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-cor-frequency-continuum",
        "exact_quote": "Let $q,D,\\Xi_D,\\mathcal I$ be as in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:frequency-block\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:frequency-block\">17</a>. Suppose that $\\mu_{a,b,k}(\\xi)$ is constant in $(a,b)$ on every cell of one finite rectangular partition, independent of $\\xi$ and common to all $k$. Suppose its values on these cells are bounded measurable functions of $\\xi$, and that $$\\label{eq:frequency-continuous-condition}\n |\\mu_{a,b,k}(\\xi)|\\leq C_1,\n \\qquad\n \\sum_k\\int_{\\Xi_D}|\\mu_{a,b,k}(\\xi)|^2\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\gamma(\\xi)\n \\leq C_1^2.$$ Define $$\\label{eq:frequency-continuous-kernel}\n \\mathcal K_{a,b}(t)\n   =\\int_{\\Xi_D}\\sum_{k\\in\\mathcal I}\n          \\mu_{a,b,k}(\\xi)\\mathop{\\mathrm{Dil}}_{L_k}H_\\xi(t)\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\gamma(\\xi).$$ Then, for complex $G_v\\in L^3(\\mathbb R^2)$, $$\\label{eq:frequency-continuous-estimate}\n |\\Lambda_{\\mathcal K}(G_0,G_1,G_2)|\n       \\leq C(q,C_1)\\prod_{v=0}^2\\|G_v\\|_3.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-082.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-frequency-continuum",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-gaussian-envelope",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $c>0$ and that a measurable kernel $f$ satisfies $|f(z)|\\le e^{-cz^2}w(z)$, where $w\\ge0$ and $\\|w\\|_5\\le\\delta$. Then, for every $L>0$, $$\\int_{\\mathbb R}|F(x+t,y)G(x,y+t)|\\,|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Dil}}_L f(t)|\\,\\,\\mathrm dt\n \\le C_c\\delta\\,\\mathcal M_{F,G}(x,y)\n \\label{eq:gaussian-envelope}$$ at every point where the right side is finite. In particular, a bound $|f(z)|\\le A e^{-cz^2}$ gives <a href=\"#eq:gaussian-envelope\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:gaussian-envelope\">[eq:gaussian-envelope]</a> with $C_c\\delta$ replaced by $C_cA$.",
        "source_id": "PM-082.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-gaussian-envelope",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that $c>0$ and that a measurable kernel $f$ satisfies $|f(z)|\\le e^{-cz^2}w(z)$, where $w\\ge0$ and $\\|w\\|_5\\le\\delta$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-rough-family",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $A\\ge1$ and $c>0$. Let $n\\ge1$, and let $\\mathcal I\\subset\\mathbb Z$ be finite. For $k\\in\\mathcal I$, let $K_{a,b,k}\\colon\\mathbb R\\to\\mathbb C$ and $m_{a,b,k}\\in\\{0,1,2,\\ldots\\}$ be finite-valued step choices in $(a,b)$, with $K_{a,b,k}=0$ whenever $m_{a,b,k}=0$. When $m_{a,b,k}\\ge1$, suppose that $K_{a,b,k}$ is $C^1$ outside a set of at most $A m_{a,b,k}$ points and that, outside that set, $$|K_{a,b,k}(z)|+|K_{a,b,k}'(z)|\\le A e^{-cz^2}.\n \\label{eq:rough-family-decay}$$ Assume also that $$\\int_{\\mathbb R}z^\\ell K_{a,b,k}(z)\\,\\,\\mathrm dz=0\n \\quad(\\ell=0,1,2),\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{k\\in\\mathcal I}m_{a,b,k}\\le An,\n \\quad m_{a,b,k}\\le A\\sqrt n.\n \\label{eq:rough-family-hypotheses}$$ For $$\\mathcal K_{a,b}(t)\n =\\sum_{k\\in\\mathcal I}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Dil}}_{2^k}K_{a,b,k}(t),$$ and smooth compactly supported $G_0,G_1,G_2$, one has $$|\\Lambda_{\\mathcal K}(G_0,G_1,G_2)|\n \\le C_{A,c}\\sqrt n\\log(2+n)\\prod_{v=0}^2\\|G_v\\|_3.\n \\label{eq:rough-family-bound}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-082.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-rough-family",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $A\\ge1$ and $c>0$. Let $n\\ge1$, and let $\\mathcal I\\subset\\mathbb Z$ be finite. When $m_{a,b,k}\\ge1$, suppose that $K_{a,b,k}$ is $C^1$ outside a set of at most $A m_{a,b,k}$ points and that, outside that set, $$|K_{a,b,k}(z)|+|K_{a,b,k}'(z)|\\le A e^{-cz^2}.\n \\label{eq:rough-family-decay}$$ Assume also that $$\\int_{\\mathbb R}z^\\ell K_{a,b,k}(z)\\,\\,\\mathrm dz=0\n \\quad(\\ell=0,1,2),\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{k\\in\\mathcal I}m_{a,b,k}\\le An,\n \\quad m_{a,b,k}\\le A\\sqrt n.\n \\label{eq:rough-family-hypotheses}$$ For $$\\mathcal K_{a,b}(t)\n =\\sum_{k\\in\\mathcal I}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Dil}}_{2^k}K_{a,b,k}(t),$$ and smooth compactly supported $G_0,G_1,G_2$, one has $$|\\Lambda_{\\mathcal K}(G_0,G_1,G_2)|\n \\le C_{A,c}\\sqrt n\\log(2+n)\\prod_{v=0}^2\\|G_v\\|_3.\n \\label{eq:rough-family-bound}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-082.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $A\\ge1$ and $c>0$. Let $n\\ge1$, and let $\\mathcal I\\subset\\mathbb Z$ be finite. When $m_{a,b,k}\\ge1$, suppose that $K_{a,b,k}$ is $C^1$ outside a set of at most $A m_{a,b,k}$ points and that, outside that set, $$|K_{a,b,k}(z)|+|K_{a,b,k}'(z)|\\le A e^{-cz^2}.\n \\label{eq:rough-family-decay}$$ Assume also that $$\\int_{\\mathbb R}z^\\ell K_{a,b,k}(z)\\,\\,\\mathrm dz=0\n \\quad(\\ell=0,1,2),\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{k\\in\\mathcal I}m_{a,b,k}\\le An,\n \\quad m_{a,b,k}\\le A\\sqrt n.\n \\label{eq:rough-family-hypotheses}$$ For $$\\mathcal K_{a,b}(t)\n =\\sum_{k\\in\\mathcal I}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Dil}}_{2^k}K_{a,b,k}(t),$$ and smooth compactly supported $G_0,G_1,G_2$, one has $$|\\Lambda_{\\mathcal K}(G_0,G_1,G_2)|\n \\le C_{A,c}\\sqrt n\\log(2+n)\\prod_{v=0}^2\\|G_v\\|_3.\n \\label{eq:rough-family-bound}$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-endpoint-errors",
        "exact_quote": "At each $(a,b)$, choose at most $n$ pairwise disjoint open radius intervals $(\\varepsilon_j,R_j)$ with $0<\\varepsilon_j<R_j<\\infty$, and complex coefficients $\\beta_j$ with $|\\beta_j|\\le1$. Suppose that these choices form a finite-valued step function of $(a,b)$. Then the kernel $$\\mathcal E_{a,b}(t)\n =\\sum_j\\beta_j\n       \\bigl(E_{\\varepsilon_j}(t)-E_{R_j}(t)\\bigr)$$ satisfies $$|\\Lambda_{\\mathcal E}(G_0,G_1,G_2)|\n \\le C\\sqrt n\\log(2+n)\\prod_{v=0}^2\\|G_v\\|_3\n \\label{eq:endpoint-error-bound}$$ for smooth compactly supported inputs.",
        "source_id": "PM-082.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-endpoint-errors",
          "exact_quote": "At each $(a,b)$, choose at most $n$ pairwise disjoint open radius intervals $(\\varepsilon_j,R_j)$ with $0<\\varepsilon_j<R_j<\\infty$, and complex coefficients $\\beta_j$ with $|\\beta_j|\\le1$. Then the kernel $$\\mathcal E_{a,b}(t)\n =\\sum_j\\beta_j\n       \\bigl(E_{\\varepsilon_j}(t)-E_{R_j}(t)\\bigr)$$ satisfies $$|\\Lambda_{\\mathcal E}(G_0,G_1,G_2)|\n \\le C\\sqrt n\\log(2+n)\\prod_{v=0}^2\\|G_v\\|_3\n \\label{eq:endpoint-error-bound}$$ for smooth compactly supported inputs.",
          "source_id": "PM-082.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-maximal",
        "exact_quote": "For complex $F,G\\in L^3(\\mathbb R^2)$, define $$B_*(F,G)(x,y)\n =\\sup_{0<\\varepsilon<R<\\infty}|B_{\\varepsilon,R}(F,G)(x,y)|$$ on the common full-measure set of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:finite-truncations\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:finite-truncations\">3</a>, and set it to zero elsewhere. <!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Annular triangular Hilbert transform** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For complex functions $F,G\in L^3(\mathbb R^2)$ and $0<\varepsilon<R<\infty$, define the annular triangular Hilbert transform by $$\label{eq:bilinear-truncation} B_{\varepsilon,R}(F,G)(x,y) =\int_{\varepsilon<\|t\|<R}F(x+t,y)G(x,y+t)\,\frac{\,\mathrm dt}{t}.$$
- **Annular variation** ([source](#eq:variation-definition)): For $r>2$, its annular variation is $$\label{eq:variation-definition} V_r(F,G)(x,y) =\sup_{\substack{J\ge1\,,\ 0<t_0<\cdots<t_J\\t_j\in\mathbb Q}} \left(\sum_{j=1}^J \|B_{t_{j-1},t_j}(F,G)(x,y)\|^r\right)^{1/r}.$$
- **Disjoint-annulus count operator** ([source](#eq:count-definition)): $$\label{eq:count-definition} \mathcal S_n(F,G) =\sup\left\{\sum_{j=1}^m\|B_{\varepsilon_j,R_j}(F,G)\|: \begin{array}{l} 0\le m\le n,\quad 0<\varepsilon_j<R_j,\quad \varepsilon_j,R_j\in\mathbb Q,\\ (\varepsilon_j,R_j)\text{ pairwise disjoint} \end{array}\right\}.$$ Intervals may share endpoints, and the empty sum is zero. As in the variation, the entire choice is made separately at every output point.
- **Step choice** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A *step choice* is a choice from a finite menu that is constant on each cell of a partition of $\mathbb R^2$ by finitely many horizontal and vertical lines. Unbounded cells are allowed.
- **Matrix energy and support** ([source](#eq:row-star)): Set $$\Phi(P)=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(P^{3/2}),\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{supp}}P=(\ker P)^\perp \quad(P\ge0).$$
- **Row star** ([source](#eq:row-star)): The two matrices incident to vertex $v$ form its *row star* $$\label{eq:row-star} R_v=[\,W_v\quad W_{v-1}^*\,]: H_{v+1}\oplus H_{v-1}\longrightarrow H_v.$$
- **Star Gram matrices** ([source](#eq:star-grams)): We use both Gram matrices $$\label{eq:star-grams} T_v=R_v^*R_v,\qquad S_v=R_vR_v^*.$$
- **Supported matrix direction** ([source](#sec:matrix)): A matrix is *supported on $P$* if it vanishes on $\ker P$ on both sides.
- **Supported Hessian quadratic cost** ([source](#sec:matrix)): For Hermitian matrices $K,L$ supported on $P$, define $$\label{eq:hessian-form} \mathcal B_P(K,L)=\frac32\operatorname{Re} \left\langle K, (\mathsf L_{\sqrt P}+\mathsf R_{\sqrt P})^{-1}L \right\rangle_{\mathrm{HS}}, \qquad b_P(K)=\mathcal B_P(K,K).$$
- **Smooth annular windows** ([source](#eq:smooth-kernels)): Define the odd windows and their scale integrals by $$\label{eq:smooth-kernels} k_s(t)=\frac{t}{\sqrt s}g_s(t),\qquad I_{\varepsilon,R}(t)=\int_{\varepsilon^2}^{R^2} (k_s*k_s*k_s)(t)\,\frac{\,\mathrm ds}{s}.$$
- **Endpoint error kernel** ([source](#pm-h-23)): For $\rho>0$ define $$\label{eq:endpoint-kernel} E_\rho(t)=\frac{P(\|t\|/\rho)-c_0\mathbf1_{\{\|t\|>\rho\}}}{t},$$ with the continuous value zero at $t=0$.
- **Modulated Gaussian frequency windows** ([source](#sec:frequency)): Define $$\label{eq:frequency-windows} \ell_\xi(z)=D^{-1}\frac{\,\mathrm d}{\,\mathrm dz} \bigl(g_q(z)e^{i\xi z}\bigr), \qquad H_\xi=\ell_\xi*\ell_\xi*\ell_\xi.$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-common-truncations** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-truncations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-truncations): Apply Hölder and Minkowski, exhaust by countably many annuli, and use absolute continuity of the integral. -> Common representatives and continuous finite truncation endpoints.
- **step-line-maximal** [claim](#stmt-lem-line-maximal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-line-maximal): Use a disjoint interval covering argument, distribution integration, and Gaussian shell decompositions. -> Line maximal bounds and Gaussian domination.
- **step-supported-hessian** [claim](#stmt-lem-hessian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hessian): Differentiate the positive square root on a fixed support through its Sylvester equation and diagonalize. -> Explicit positive Hessian metric and metric Cauchy–Schwarz.
- **step-order-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-order): Combine kernel inclusion, square-root monotonicity, inverse order, and positive-definite regularization. -> Comparison of supported costs on nested supports.
- **step-insertion-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-insertion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-insertion): Use singular vectors and row and column norm bounds for the compressed insertion. -> Energy bound for an inserted Hermitian cost.
- **step-average-convexity** [claim](#stmt-lem-joint-convexity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-joint-convexity): Compare the square root of the averaged Gram with the average square root and use an inverse variational formula. -> Joint convexity under probability averaging.
- **step-rank-change** [claim](#stmt-lem-rank-change) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rank-change): Regularize the Gram, integrate the derivative along an affine path, and pass uniformly to the unregularized limit. -> Rank-tolerant energy variation and entry gradient bounds.
- **step-mixed-trace** [claim](#stmt-lem-mixed-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mixed-trace): Embed the cycle in a Hermitian block matrix, compare all costs in a common metric, bound the cubic trace, and rotate the complex phase. -> Dimension-free control of the cyclic trace.
- **step-heat-identities** [claim](#stmt-lem-heat-identities) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-heat-identities): Differentiate Gaussian weights using fixed row or column supports, justify domination, and integrate in plane charts. -> Pointwise and plane-integrated heat identities.
- **step-plane-dissipation** [claim](#stmt-lem-plane-dissipation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-plane-dissipation): Compare neighboring column costs with a row cost and absorb the mixed terms for the specified rates. -> Strict dissipation at comparable Gaussian variances.
- **step-initial-energy** [claim](#stmt-lem-initial-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-initial-energy): Bound the Gaussian grid mass, apply weighted Hölder, and integrate over the endpoint centers. -> Uniform initial edge and star energy bounds.
- **step-single-edge** [claim](#stmt-lem-single-edge-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-single-edge-energy): Delete the other star block, eliminate the mixed derivative, integrate exact dissipation, and compare with the original column cost. -> Single-edge integrated cost control for arbitrary positive rates.
- **step-repeated-masks** [claim](#stmt-lem-repeated-dissipation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-repeated-dissipation): Bound each energy jump entrywise, average Gaussian weights, apply line maximal estimates, and telescope between switches. -> Repeated-mask dissipation with quadratic mixed dependence on the dual norm.
- **step-smooth-annuli** [claim](#stmt-prop-smooth-switches) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-repeated-dissipation-2): Insert Gaussian derivative windows into the mixed trace inequality, narrow the row variance, integrate centers and scales, rescale the dual array, and pass by Riemann sums. -> Smooth annular bound uniform in step choices and endpoints.
- **step-frequency-boundary** [claim](#stmt-lem-frequency-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-frequency-boundary): Compute the Gaussian covariance under a normalized center measure and dominate it by coordinate Gaussian averages and iterated maximal operators. -> A scale-independent majorant for mixed boundary energies.
- **step-frequency-block** [claim](#stmt-prop-frequency-block) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-frequency-boundary-2): Refine oscillatory insertions by probability averages, use single-edge dissipation for column costs, sum slab boundary costs using coefficient squares, and recover the cyclic form. -> Uniform finite frequency-block estimate.
- **step-frequency-continuum** [claim](#stmt-cor-frequency-continuum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-frequency-continuum): Perform bounded frequency integration before taking lattice Riemann limits, then extend by an integrable majorant and smooth approximation. -> Continuous frequency-block bound for general complex inputs.
- **step-gaussian-envelope** [claim](#stmt-lem-gaussian-envelope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gaussian-envelope): Apply Hölder after dilation and bound the resulting Gaussian averages by directional maximal functions. -> Pointwise maximal domination of the absolute kernel integral.
- **step-rough-family** [claim](#stmt-prop-rough-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rough-family): Build a Gaussian-weighted primitive, derive bounded-variation Fourier estimates, truncate frequency with a summable envelope error, and sum the frequency-block bounds. -> Rough-family bound with square-root and logarithmic count dependence.
- **step-odd-family** [claim](#stmt-cor-odd-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-odd-family): Subtract a normalized odd function to remove the first moment, then expand the subtracted term by a summable dilation identity. -> Rough-family bound assuming oddness instead of the moment conditions.
- **step-endpoint-errors** [claim](#stmt-prop-endpoint-errors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-endpoint-errors): Group endpoints by dyadic radius, pair endpoints inside each group, exploit disjoint total length, and separate groups according to their counts. -> Uniform bound for the sum of all endpoint errors.
- **step-count-estimate** [claim](#stmt-thm-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-endpoint-errors-2): Assemble smooth and endpoint bounds, approximate measurable finite menus by rectangular step choices, dualize finite maxima, and use density and monotone exhaustion. -> Disjoint-annulus count estimate for general complex inputs.
- **step-full-variation** [claim](#stmt-thm-variation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-endpoint-errors-3): Sort increments by decreasing size, group ranks dyadically, apply the count estimate, and sum the convergent rank series. -> Full pointwise annular variation bound.
- **step-maximal-extension** [claim](#stmt-cor-maximal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-maximal): Identify the maximal function with the single-annulus count and use bilinearity and density to prove continuity and uniqueness. -> Hard maximal bound and continuous extension.
- **step-bilinear-principal-value** [claim](#stmt-cor-bilinear-pv) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-bilinear-pv): Establish smooth cancellation, bound tail oscillation by maximal perturbations, pass through smooth approximation, and use dominated convergence. -> Joint bilinear principal value and maximal norm-tail convergence.
- **step-flat-scalar** [claim](#stmt-cor-scalar-pv) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-scalar-pv): Use absolute integrability and Fubini to identify the scalar form with a bilinear pairing, then pair the joint norm limit. -> Uniform flat scalar bound and unique trilinear principal value.
- **step-simplex-equivalence** [claim](#stmt-prop-flat-equivalence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-flat-equivalence): Apply invertible determinant-one coordinate maps preserving input norms and both truncation endpoints. -> Simplex scalar conclusions and equality of optimal scalar constants.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-common-representatives**: Finite annular truncations share a common exceptional null set, are absolutely convergent elsewhere, and depend continuously on their endpoints there.
- **invariant-annulus-count**: The norm of the pointwise disjoint-annulus count is bounded with square-root growth and a logarithmic factor in the permitted number of annuli.
- **invariant-full-variation**: The annular variation satisfies the stated bilinear norm bound while allowing the partition to depend on the output point.
- **invariant-frequency-coefficients**: The frequency-block estimate requires both uniform coefficient control and a pointwise square-integral bound over scales and frequencies, together with the mesh condition.
- **invariant-rough-kernel-cancellation**: The rough-family estimate combines Gaussian size and derivative control, bounded jump counts, and vanishing moments; the odd-family corollary supplies the stated alternative cancellation hypothesis.
- **invariant-joint-principal-value**: The bilinear principal value exists almost everywhere and in norm, and the supremum of the entire truncation tail converges in norm.
- **invariant-scalar-equivalence**: The flat and simplex scalar formulations have equal optimal uniform finite-truncation constants, and their principal values transfer through norm-preserving coordinate maps.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-endpoint-variation**: Extend the variation theorem to the threshold exponent or below it. -> The rank-summation argument no longer supplies the convergent series used to prove the variation bound; this does not establish a counterexample at those exponents.
- **anti-overlapping-annuli**: Apply the count estimate to an arbitrary collection of overlapping radius intervals. -> Overlaps remove the disjointness used to control mask amplitudes and to pair endpoint contributions within a scale group.
- **anti-frequency-supremum-only**: Invoke the frequency-block estimate from coefficient boundedness alone. -> Uniform boundedness alone does not supply the scale-frequency square-integral control used to sum mixed boundary energies.
- **anti-arbitrary-heat-rates**: Assert strict decrease of the summed star energy for arbitrary positive variance rates. -> The general plane-dissipation inequality does not guarantee negative coefficients for every choice of rates.
- **anti-unsupported-hessian-directions**: Evaluate the supported Hessian inverse on unrestricted Hermitian directions at a singular Gram matrix. -> The inverse is defined on matrices acting on the support; directions meeting the kernel need not belong to its domain.
- **anti-effective-stopping-rule**: Turn joint principal-value convergence into a certified numerical stopping rule for arbitrary inputs. -> The convergence statement gives no computable tail modulus or discretization-error bound for general inputs.
- **anti-optimal-constant-identification**: Identify the common optimal scalar constant with the optimal maximal or variation constant. -> Equality of the scalar constants does not identify constants for pointwise suprema or variation.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The principal results concern arbitrary complex inputs in continuous function spaces, pointwise suprema over partitions, and joint principal-value limits. The source states: “The supremum is pointwise: the partition may depend on $(x,y)$, and there is no restriction on the number of endpoints within a dyadic scale interval.” A finite enumeration cannot decide these analytic assertions. The finite matrix lemmas support the proof but do not reduce the full variation or principal-value conclusions to a finite constructive computation.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The bounded extension of the continuous transform specifies mathematical behavior but supplies no executable evaluator or numerical error contract. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Gaussian evolution controls analytic matrix energies; it does not construct a game simulation, physical state update, or collision model. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The principal-value theorem can inform mathematical specifications, but it provides no finite stopping certificate for an evaluation tool. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The passage from finite arrays to continuous inputs uses limiting Riemann sums and smooth approximation rather than a finite algorithm with a prescribed accuracy guarantee. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Row stars and Gram matrices organize the proof's algebraic quantities, but no storage representation, update interface, or data-structure performance contract is established. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Dimension independence of the mixed trace inequality concerns its analytic constant and supplies no accelerator construction, memory bound, or execution schedule. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Probability averaging of supported matrices establishes a convexity inequality, with no cryptographic primitive, adversary model, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The supported Hessian is an analytic cost for a specific matrix energy; the paper constructs no learning objective, training procedure, or generalization guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Strict dissipation is proved for specified Gaussian variance rates, without constructing an optimization algorithm or establishing convergence to an optimizer. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The count parameter measures disjoint annular radius intervals chosen at output points; it supplies no model of messages, replicas, tasks, or scheduling resources. |
| other (harmonic analysis and operator theory) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The paper constructs the annular triangular Hilbert transform from transverse translations and an odd kernel, then controls its pointwise annular variation and obtains a bounded bilinear principal-value extension. This is a concrete analytic operator construction that can guide a mathematical specification; finite numerical evaluation requires additional error analysis. |

          "source_object": "stmt-lem-rank-change"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-variation-exponent** (hypothesis): The annular variation bound requires $r>2$. The supplied argument does not establish the endpoint or lower variation exponents; this omission is not a counterexample. Blocked use: ['Claiming quadratic or total annular variation bounds from this theorem.']
- **limit-symmetric-lebesgue-regime** (hypothesis): The asserted operator bounds concern the stated symmetric Lebesgue exponents. Estimates at other input and output exponents require separate justification. Blocked use: ['Applying the variation, maximal, or principal-value bounds to arbitrary Lebesgue exponent tuples.']
- **limit-symmetric-truncation-geometry** (formal_scope): The transform uses transverse coordinate translations and symmetric annular truncations. Independently cutting off the positive and negative halves changes the operator under consideration. Blocked use: ['Transferring the conclusions to asymmetric cutoffs or unrelated translation geometries.']
- **limit-common-representatives** (edge_case): Finite annular integrals are asserted to converge absolutely and vary continuously outside a common null set. Comparisons involving different endpoints must respect that common representative convention. Blocked use: ['Claiming absolute convergence at every output point for arbitrary admissible representatives.', 'Combining independently chosen exceptional sets without establishing a common full-measure domain.']
- **limit-disjoint-annulus-budget** (hypothesis): The count quantity ranges over bounded-length lists of pairwise disjoint radius intervals. Shared endpoints and the empty list are allowed, but overlapping interiors are outside its definition. Blocked use: ['Using the count bound for arbitrary overlapping collections of annuli.', 'Interpreting the count parameter as a bound on computational concurrency.']
- **limit-supported-hessian-domain** (hypothesis): The Hessian inverse acts on supported Hermitian directions. Fixed-support differentiation does not authorize unrestricted differentiation through a rank change. Blocked use: ['Applying the supported inverse to a direction meeting the kernel.', 'Using the fixed-support Hessian formula as a replacement for the separate rank-change argument.']
- **limit-probability-average-support** (hypothesis): The averaging lemma assumes a common support almost everywhere and integrability of the matrices and directions. It permits an infinite right-hand cost and therefore does not by itself guarantee a finite averaged-cost bound. Blocked use: ['Dropping the common-support or integrability assumptions from the averaging lemma.', 'Treating an infinite upper bound as finite cost control.']
- **limit-hermitian-trace-insertions** (hypothesis): The mixed trace lemma assumes Hermitian insertions. The extension supplied for complex diagonal insertions splits them into real and imaginary diagonal parts and uses the separately stated bound. Blocked use: ['Applying the Hermitian insertion bound directly to arbitrary complex insertions.', 'Assigning the Hermitian constant to the complex diagonal extension without accounting for its expansion.']
- **limit-heat-dissipation-rates** (hypothesis): The general heat identities allow arbitrary positive rates, while strict dissipation of the summed star energy is obtained for the specified comparable rates. The isolated single-edge estimate has its own hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Asserting strict summed-star dissipation solely from positivity of all variance rates.', 'Using the single-edge dissipation identity while retaining both star blocks.']
- **limit-initial-energy-mesh** (hypothesis): The uniform initial-energy estimate requires Gaussian variances no smaller than the squared mesh. Its stated uniform bound cannot simply be carried below that threshold. Blocked use: ['Invoking the mesh-uniform initial-energy constant for arbitrarily narrow Gaussians on a fixed lattice.']
- **limit-smooth-switch-linearization** (hypothesis): The smooth-annulus proposition starts with compactly supported smooth inputs, step choices of disjoint intervals, and bounded complex coefficients. Extension to general inputs and measurable choices is a later argument. Blocked use: ['Invoking the smooth proposition alone for unrestricted measurable choices or arbitrary rough inputs.', 'Dropping coefficient control or disjointness in the repeated-mask argument.']
- **limit-frequency-block-contract** (hypothesis): The finite frequency-block estimate requires a finite nonempty scale set, finite arrays, measurable coefficients satisfying both coefficient inequalities at every output index pair, and the prescribed mesh restriction. Blocked use: ['Replacing the joint scale-frequency square-integral hypothesis by coefficient boundedness alone.', 'Applying the finite frequency-block estimate at an inadmissible mesh.', 'Claiming the proposition directly covers an infinite scale set.']
- **limit-continuum-passage** (approximation): The continuous frequency-block corollary assumes a common finite rectangular partition for its coefficient choices. Its passage through Riemann sums and smooth approximation supplies no quantitative discretization-error estimate. Blocked use: ['Claiming a certified quadrature tolerance from the continuum passage.', 'Discarding the common rectangular step-partition assumption when applying this corollary directly.']
- **limit-gaussian-envelope-finiteness** (hypothesis): The pointwise Gaussian-envelope estimate requires the stated Gaussian-weighted envelope and applies where its maximal-function right-hand side is finite. Blocked use: ['Using the pointwise bound at a point where its majorant is infinite as a finite error certificate.', 'Applying the estimate to an unrestricted kernel without the envelope hypothesis.']
- **limit-rough-family-cancellation-and-counts** (hypothesis): The rough-family estimate requires Gaussian size and derivative control away from controlled discontinuities, finite-valued step choices, and both total and per-scale count restrictions. Oddness replaces the moment conditions while retaining the other hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Applying the rough-family estimate to arbitrary discontinuous kernels.', 'Dropping the per-scale count bound because a total count bound is available.', 'Treating oddness as a replacement for decay, regularity, or count control.']
- **limit-grouped-endpoint-errors** (hypothesis): The endpoint-error estimate assumes pointwise disjoint annuli, bounded coefficients, and finite-valued step choices. Grouping comparable endpoints exploits that disjointness rather than treating every endpoint independently. Blocked use: ['Applying the grouped error estimate to arbitrary overlapping annuli.', 'Assuming the same estimate follows by summing separate endpoint bounds without the grouping argument.']
- **limit-qualitative-principal-value-tail** (nonconstructive): Joint almost-everywhere and norm convergence, including maximal-tail convergence, is established for admissible inputs. A computable tail modulus or universal numerical stopping criterion is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Certifying a numerical approximation from qualitative convergence alone.', 'Assigning a convergence rate to arbitrary admissible inputs without additional regularity or tail estimates.']
- **limit-scalar-optimal-constants** (formal_scope): Equality of the optimal flat and simplex scalar constants does not assert equality with the maximal or variation constants. Blocked use: ['Identifying the common optimal scalar constant with the optimal pointwise maximal or variation constant.']
- **limit-finite-certification-scope** (formal_scope): The principal conclusions quantify over continuous function spaces, pointwise partition suprema, and joint limits. Checking a finite collection of sampled inputs cannot certify those full analytic assertions. Blocked use: ['Presenting agreement on finite examples as verification of the full variation or principal-value theorem.', 'Interpreting dimension-free analytic bounds as runtime or memory-complexity guarantees.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the annular <i>r</i>-variation estimate for the triangular Hilbert transform from complex $`L^3\times L^3`$ to <i>L</i><sup>3/2</sup> for every <i>r</i> &gt; 2. The partitions may depend on the output point and range over all positive scales. The estimate yields the two-endpoint maximal bound and joint almost-everywhere and norm principal values, and resolves the symmetric scalar triangular Hilbert transform problem.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

For complex functions $F,G\in L^3(\mathbb R^2)$ and $0<\varepsilon<R<\infty$, define the annular triangular Hilbert transform by <a id="eq:bilinear-truncation"></a>$$\label{eq:bilinear-truncation}
 B_{\varepsilon,R}(F,G)(x,y)
 =\int_{\varepsilon<|t|<R}F(x+t,y)G(x,y+t)\,\frac{\,\mathrm dt}{t}.$$ The two inputs are translated along different coordinate directions. Every finite integral is absolutely convergent outside one common null set; we use its usual representative there and set all quantities to zero on the exceptional set. Lemma <a href="#lem:finite-truncations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:finite-truncations">3</a> justifies this convention and continuity in the endpoints.

<a id="eq:variation-definition"></a>
For $r>2$, its annular variation is $$\label{eq:variation-definition}
 V_r(F,G)(x,y)
 =\sup_{\substack{J\ge1\,,\ 0<t_0<\cdots<t_J\\t_j\in\mathbb Q}}
    \left(\sum_{j=1}^J
          |B_{t_{j-1},t_j}(F,G)(x,y)|^r\right)^{1/r}.$$ The supremum is pointwise: the partition may depend on $(x,y)$, and there is no restriction on the number of endpoints within a dyadic scale interval. The use of rational endpoints makes measurability immediate and, by endpoint continuity, does not change the supremum.

<a id="stmt-thm-variation"></a>
<div id="thm:variation" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Full annular variation). *For every real $r>2$ there is a finite constant $C_r$ such that $$\|V_r(F,G)\|_{L^{3/2}(\mathbb R^2)}
 \le C_r\|F\|_{L^3(\mathbb R^2)}\|G\|_{L^3(\mathbb R^2)}$$ for all complex $F,G\in L^3(\mathbb R^2)$.*

</div>

Taking a single increment gives the maximal estimate with the supremum over both hard truncation endpoints. In Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">9</a> we prove that the joint principal value $$B(F,G)=\lim_{\substack{\varepsilon\downarrow0\\R\uparrow\infty}}
                   B_{\varepsilon,R}(F,G)$$ exists almost everywhere and in $L^{3/2}$. In fact, the supremum of $|B_{\varepsilon,R}(F,G)-B(F,G)|$ over $0<\varepsilon<1/m$, $R>m$, tends to zero in $L^{3/2}$.

<a id="eq:intro-simplex"></a>
Pairing the bilinear output with a third $L^3$ input gives a scalar form. Its symmetric, or simplex, coordinates are $$\label{eq:intro-simplex}
 \Lambda_{\varepsilon,R}(G_0,G_1,G_2)
 =\iiint_{\varepsilon<|a+b+c|<R}
  G_0(a,b)G_1(b,c)G_2(c,a)
             \frac{\,\mathrm da\,\mathrm db\,\mathrm dc}{a+b+c}.$$ The three equal exponents $3$ form the symmetric point of the scalar Hölder relation $1/p_0+1/p_1+1/p_2=1$. We obtain a uniform bound by $C\prod_v\|G_v\|_3$ and the joint scalar principal value for every complex $L^3$ triple. Proposition <a href="#prop:flat-equivalence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:flat-equivalence">27</a> gives the norm-preserving coordinate maps and equality of the optimal constants for the flat and simplex scalar formulations. In particular, this proves the symmetric scalar estimate in Thiele’s Problem 13 in (Grafakos et al. 2017, sec. 9) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[GrafakosEtAl2017](#ref-GrafakosEtAl2017)].

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and related work

The flat operator occurs in Demeter and Thiele’s study of bilinear averages for two commuting transformations (Demeter and Thiele 2010, sec. 6) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[DemeterThiele2010](#ref-DemeterThiele2010)]. The scalar form <a href="#eq:intro-simplex" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-simplex">[eq:intro-simplex]</a> is a basic entangled singular integral: each input sees one pair of the three variables, and the pairs form a cycle. This dependence differs from the bipartite configurations in many multilinear estimates. Kovač, Thiele and Zorin-Kranich established estimates for a Walsh model with two general inputs and a third input of specified structure (Kovač, Thiele, and Zorin-Kranich 2015, Theorem 1.7) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[KovacThieleZorinKranich2015](#ref-KovacThieleZorinKranich2015)]. Their paper also records the cyclic trace formulation and the equivalence of the flat and simplex forms.

Quantitative cancellation over a finite range of scales was developed in several stages. Tao proved sublogarithmic bounds for one-dimensional multilinear Hilbert transforms (Tao 2016) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Tao2016Cancellation](#ref-Tao2016Cancellation)], and Zorin-Kranich obtained sublogarithmic cancellation for simplex Hilbert transforms (Zorin-Kranich 2017) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[ZorinKranich2017](#ref-ZorinKranich2017)]. Durcik, Kovač, Škreb and Thiele proved square-function and norm-variation estimates that yield square-root cancellation for the triangular form over finitely many smooth scales (Durcik et al. 2019, Corollary 5) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[DurcikKovacSkrebThiele2019](#ref-DurcikKovacSkrebThiele2019)]. Durcik, Kovač and Thiele obtained power-type cancellation for hard truncations (Durcik, Kovač, and Thiele 2019, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[DurcikKovacThiele2019](#ref-DurcikKovacThiele2019)]. Permuting and interpolating their $(4,4,2)$ bounds gives a $\sqrt{\log(R/\varepsilon)}$ bound at the symmetric tuple. These estimates retain a dependence on the scale range. Moreover, norm-variation estimates for a fixed list of scales do not place the supremum over output-dependent partitions inside the output norm as in <a href="#eq:variation-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:variation-definition">[eq:variation-definition]</a>.

Other results gain cancellation by averaging a direction parameter (Durcik and Roos 2021; Lin and Slavíková 2026) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[DurcikRoos2021](#ref-DurcikRoos2021), [LinSlavikova2026](#ref-LinSlavikova2026)] or by introducing curvature (Christ, Durcik, and Roos 2021; Hsu and Lin 2026) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[ChristDurcikRoos2021](#ref-ChristDurcikRoos2021), [HsuLin2026](#ref-HsuLin2026)]. Those mechanisms are distinct from the flat problem considered here. The broader role of variation in strengthening maximal estimates and controlling convergence is illustrated by Lépingle’s martingale inequality (Lépingle 1976) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Lepingle1976](#ref-Lepingle1976)], the variational inequalities of Jones, Seeger and Wright (Jones, Seeger, and Wright 2008) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[JonesSeegerWright2008](#ref-JonesSeegerWright2008)], and the variation-norm Carleson theorem (Oberlin et al. 2012) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[OberlinSeegerTaoThieleWright2012](#ref-OberlinSeegerTaoThieleWright2012)]. These results provide context rather than an input to our proof.

The energy method here is related to telescoping arguments for entangled forms: Kovač’s twisted paraproduct and Bellman-function framework (Kovač 2012, 2011) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Kovac2012Twisted](#ref-Kovac2012Twisted), [Kovac2011](#ref-Kovac2011)], and Durcik’s continuous Gaussian telescoping (Durcik 2015, 2017) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Durcik2015](#ref-Durcik2015), [Durcik2017](#ref-Durcik2017)]. The obstruction presented by a triangular cycle is discussed in (Kovač 2011, sec. 6.2) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Kovac2011](#ref-Kovac2011)]. We work with the finite matrix energy $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(P^{3/2})$ for $P\ge0$, establish a mixed trace inequality, and compare its heat derivatives at neighboring vertices. All matrix and heat estimates required below are proved in this paper.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The count estimate and the new ingredients
